The 2-categorical structure of complete intersections

As previously mentioned, there are quiver gauge theories with 8 supercharges that do not admit a mirror symmetric 3D
{\cal N}=4
theory description. The reason for this can be recast to the fact that its HB is not a single kyperk
\ddot{\text{a}}
ler cone, but, rather, the union of two, with nontrivial intersection, such as the cases shown in figure
115
and
119
.

For classical gauge groups
G\equiv U(N),Sp(N)
or
USp(2N),SO(2N)
, the moduli space is a complete intersection, meaning it is an algebraic variety whose dimension,
d
, is given by the following expression

d\ \overset{def.}{=}\ g-r,

(933)

where
g,r
respectively denote the generators and relations between them.

For the case in which a 3D
{\cal N}=4
gauge theory,
T
, with given gauge group and suitable choice of matter content, admits a mirror dual theory,
T^{{}^{\text{V}}}
, the Hilbert series evaluated on the Coulomb branch (CB) of the former equals that of the Higgs branch (HB) of the latter,

\text{HS}\left[\text{CB}\ \left(T\right)\right]\ \equiv\ \text{HS}\left[\text{HB}\ \left(T^{{}^{\text{V}}}\right)\right],

(934)

hence

\text{HB}\left(\text{EQ}\right)\ \equiv\ \text{CB}\left(\text{MQ}\right).

(935)

Perfect realisation of ordinary mirror symmetry in (
934
) follows from the HB and CB of the dual theory both being hyperk
\ddot{\text{a}}
hler quotients. Recent developments in terms of magnetic quivers and Hasse diagrams highlighted the fact that setups where (
934
) effectively takes place involve Hasse diagrams featuring a single cone, such as in figure
117
and
118
. This feature naturally emerges from the HS calculation in the sense that (
936
) would only account for the contribution from the generators of the unique cone involved in the construction of the diagram. On the other hand, for theories whose Hasse diagram is the union of two intersecting cones, (
937
) splits into terms associated to the generators of each individual cone, together with additional subtractive terms accounting for the nontrivial intersection in between them.

Extended calculation of HS for arbitrary gauge theories with 8 supercharges has been the main focus of several recent works, mostly
[
197
,
198
,
223
]
. As shown in
[
198
]
, there are some cases in which one could trade a cone for an intersection, thereby indicating that the generators and the relations in between them encode the same information. This turns out to be of particular importance for our purposes, in particular in building the correspondence
132
132

132

A more detailed explanation of this will be provided in the next section, when comparing with
[
192
]
.
with the Drinfeld center
133
133

133

As stated in the introduction, the focus of
[
3
]
was outlining the main proposal of connecting Drinfeld centers and magnetic quivers from a theoretical point of view, while reserving a more formal mathematical treatment of the same correspondence to followup work by the same author,
[
4
,
5
]
.
.

Within the context of quiver gauge theories with 8 supercharges, the Hilbert series (HS) is a partition function counting chiral gauge-invariant operators, encoding the variety of vacua generated by such operators. The highest weight generating function (HWG), encodes the same information in a more succinct way, that is more useful to be dealing with for cases involving higher rank,
[
223
]
.

The general expression reads

\displaystyle\text{HS}_{{}_{N_{{}_{f}}}}(t;x_{{}_{1}},,...x_{{}_{k}})

\displaystyle\equiv\ \underset{j}{\sum}\ f_{{}_{j}}(x_{{}_{1}},,...x_{{}_{k}})\ t^{{}^{j}},

(936)

with
t
denoting a fugacity for the highest weight of the
SU(2)_{{}_{R}}

R
-symmetry group providing the grading for the ring of functions, while
f_{{}_{i}}(x_{{}_{1}},,...x_{{}_{k}})
are sums of characters for irreducible representations of the global symmetry group. From this follows the equivalence with (
649
) and (
650
). In the notation of
[
223
]
, this reads

\displaystyle\text{HS}_{{}_{N_{{}_{f}}}}(t;x_{{}_{1}},,...x_{{}_{k}})

\displaystyle\equiv\ \int d\mu_{{}_{G}}\ \text{HS}\left(\frac{\mathbb{C}[{\cal Q},\tilde{\cal Q}]}{\sqrt{\text{$F$-terms}\ }}\right)\ \equiv\text{HS}\left(\frac{\mathbb{C}[M,{\cal Q},\tilde{\cal Q}]}{\sqrt{\text{Rel.s}\ }}\right)

(937)

with
M,{\cal Q}
denoting mesons and baryons, respectively.

For the case of 4D
{\cal N}=2
SQCD with gauge group
G=SU(N_{{}_{c}})
and
N_{{}_{f}}
hypermultiplets in the fundamental representation of
SU(N_{{}_{c}})
, the calculation of
937
requires:

1.

solving the
F
-term equations, setting the derivatives of the superpotential to zero

2.

identifying the gauge-invariant operators.

For
{\cal N}=2
SU(2)
N_{{}_{f}}=2
, the HS is a sum of rational functions,
[
223
]
,

\displaystyle\text{HS}(t;x_{{}_{1}},x_{{}_{2}})

\displaystyle\equiv\ \int d\mu_{{}_{SU(2)}}\ F(t,z,x_{{}_{1}},x_{{}_{2}})

(938)

\displaystyle\equiv\ \text{HS}(\mathbb{C}^{{}^{2}}/\mathbb{Z}_{{}_{2}};t,x_{{}_{1}})+\text{HS}(\mathbb{C}^{{}^{2}}/\mathbb{Z}_{{}_{2}};t,x_{{}_{2}})-1

with the first two terms corresponding to the two cones and the last term denoting the intersection in between them at the origin, as shown in figure
115
. When expanding it in terms of powers of
t
, we get the Plethystic logarithm (PL),

\displaystyle\text{PL}(t;x_{{}_{1}},x_{{}_{2}})

\displaystyle\equiv\ ([2;0]+[0;2])t^{{}^{2}}-([2;2]+2[0;0])t^{{}^{4}}+...

(939)

with
[;]
denoting the characters of the corresponding representations of
SO(4)
.
The character corresponding to a certain representation can be encoded in the corresponding Dynkin label. We can therefore choose a set of fugacities to keep track of them, enabling us to rewrite (
938
) and (
939
) as a highest weight generating function

\text{HWG}\left(t;\mu_{{}_{1}},\mu_{{}_{2}}\right)\ \equiv\ \text{PE}\left[\left(\mu_{{}_{1}}^{{}^{2}}+\mu_{{}_{2}}^{{}^{2}}\right)t^{{}^{2}}-\mu_{{}_{1}}^{{}^{2}}\mu_{{}_{2}}^{{}^{2}}t^{{}^{4}}\right],

(940)

with the first two terms corresponding to the generators of the two cones, and the last (subtractive) term to their intersection in the Hasse diagram.

Key points

The main message of this section can be summarised as follows:

•

Quiver gauge theories with 8 supercharges are characterised by a structure fully specified by knowing the generators and intersections of the cones in their HB Hasse diagram. The latter is obtained by implementing quiver subtraction on MQs associated to maximal decompositions of the 5-brane webs associated to the electric quiver. As such, it admits a 2-categorical structure description.

•

The moment map plays a key role in ensuring the identification of the underlying MQs. The flatness condition it is required to satisfy defines the HS, hence the HWG function,
[
344
]
. Combining the works of
[
222
,
195
,
196
,
197
,
198
,
223
]
, this constitutes a sample realisation of homological mirror symmetry.

•

In presence of more than one cone in the Hasse diagram, the identification of the MQs whose CBs’ union equals the HB of the original theory, is a generalised statement of homological mirror symmetry.

We wish to emphasise that, while most of the above statements have been individually addressed in the literature, to the best of our knowledge, their combined application towards identifying the Drinfeld center in connection to CBs constitutes an original approach, to which Part
V
is devoted.
